{'role': 'user', 'content': [{'type': 'text', 'text': '<file name="D:\\dev\\ab-tasks\\_briefs\\anyio\\brief.md">\n# Task: fix a reported bug in `anyio`\r\n\r\n## Bug report (verbatim from the project\'s issue tracker)\r\n**CapacityLimiter can over-grant tokens (`borrowed_tokens > total_tokens`, `available_tokens` goes negative)**\r\n\r\n### Things to check first\r\n\r\n- [x] I have searched the existing issues and didn\'t find my bug already reported there\r\n\r\n- [x] I have checked that my bug is still present in the latest release\r\n\r\n\r\n### AnyIO version\r\n\r\n4.13.0\r\n\r\n### Python version\r\n\r\n3.12\r\n\r\n### What happened?\r\n\r\nOn the asyncio backend, a non-blocking acquire can succeed *while the just-freed token is already reserved for a woken waiter*, leaving the limiter with more borrowers than it has tokens. The state becomes:\r\n\r\n```\r\nborrowed_tokens  = 2\r\ntotal_tokens     = 1\r\navailable_tokens = -1      # negative\r\nborrowers        = (\'B\', \'X\')\r\n```\r\n\r\nTwo tasks then hold a single-token limiter concurrently — the exact condition the limiter exists to prevent — and `available_tokens` is negative.\r\n\r\nThis violates the documented contract of `acquire_nowait` / `acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait`:\r\n\r\n> :raises ~anyio.WouldBlock: if there are no tokens available for borrowing\r\n\r\nIn the window below there is no token available (the freed one is earmarked for the woken waiter), yet the nowait acquire succeeds instead of raising `WouldBlock`.\r\n\r\n### How can we reproduce the bug?\r\n\r\nJust anyio, no test framework: `python repro.py` prints the corrupt state and raises `AssertionError`:\r\n\r\n```python\r\nimport anyio\r\nfrom anyio import CapacityLimiter, WouldBlock\r\n\r\nasync def main():\r\n    lim = CapacityLimiter(1)                       # capacity: exactly ONE token\r\n    lim.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait("A")           # A borrows the only token\r\n\r\n    async with anyio.create_task_group() as tg:\r\n        async def b():\r\n            await lim.acquire_on_behalf_of("B")    # B waits for the token\r\n        tg.start_soon(b)\r\n\r\n        while lim.statistics().tasks_waiting != 1:  # wait until B is parked\r\n            await anyio.sleep(0)\r\n\r\n        lim.release_on_behalf_of("A")              # frees the token, notifies B\r\n        # B has been notified but has NOT resumed yet, so it is not in\r\n        # _borrowers and the wait queue is now empty. A non-blocking acquire\r\n        # in this window slips through:\r\n        try:\r\n            lim.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait("X")  # expected: WouldBlock\r\n            print("X acquired without waiting (should have raised WouldBlock)")\r\n        except WouldBlock:\r\n            print("X correctly blocked")\r\n        # leaving the task group lets B resume and add itself to _borrowers\r\n\r\n    print(f"borrowed_tokens  = {lim.borrowed_tokens}  (total_tokens = {lim.total_tokens})")\r\n    print(f"available_tokens = {lim.available_tokens}")\r\n    print(f"borrowers        = {lim.statistics().borrowers}")\r\n    assert lim.borrowed_tokens <= lim.total_tokens, "borrowed_tokens > total_tokens"\r\n\r\nanyio.run(main)\r\n```\r\n\r\nOutput:\r\n\r\n```\r\nX acquired without waiting (should have raised WouldBlock)\r\nborrowed_tokens  = 2  (total_tokens = 1)\r\navailable_tokens = -1\r\nborrowers        = (\'B\', \'X\')\r\nAssertionError: borrowed_tokens > total_tokens\r\n```\r\n### Cause\r\n\r\nIn `anyio/_backends/_asyncio.py`:\r\n\r\n- `release_on_behalf_of` removes the borrower then calls `_notify_next_waiter()`, which **pops** the next waiter out of `_wait_queue` and does `event.set()`.\r\n- The woken waiter, in `acquire_on_behalf_of`, only adds itself to `_borrowers` **after** `await event.wait()` returns:\r\n\r\n  ```python\r\n  await event.wait()\r\n  ...\r\n  self._borrowers.add(borrower)   # runs only once the waiter resumes\r\n  ```\r\n\r\n- Between the `event.set()` and the waiter resuming, `_wait_queue` is empty and the waiter is not yet in `_borrowers`. So the nowait guard\r\n\r\n  ```python\r\n  if self._wait_queue or len(self._borrowers) >= self._total_tokens:\r\n      raise WouldBlock\r\n  ```\r\n\r\n  evaluates to `False` (empty queue, `0 < 1`) and admits a second borrower for the one token.\r\n\r\n### Why `Semaphore` is not affected\r\n\r\nThe sibling `Semaphore` is immune under the identical scenario, a nowait acquire issued in the same window correctly raises `WouldBlock`, because its `release()` hands the freed slot to the woken waiter rather than leaving it generally available. `CapacityLimiter` is the one primitive whose freed-but-reserved token is briefly visible as "available" to a synchronous acquirer.\r\n\r\nA fix would close that window, e.g. by reserving the token for the woken waiter at notify time (add the borrower to `_borrowers` when `_notify_next_waiter` sets its event, rather than after it resumes), mirroring the Semaphore/Lock hand-off. The nowait guard would then correctly see no free token.\r\n\r\n## Standing instructions (identical for every run)\r\nYou are working in the repository at the current working directory. Work like a careful open-source contributor:\r\n1. Reproduce the bug first (write a minimal failing test or script) before changing code.\r\n2. Find the root cause; fix the cause, not the symptom. Keep the change minimal and in the project\'s style.\r\n3. Add or update a regression test that fails before your fix and passes after.\r\n4. Run the project\'s test suite and make it green. Do not modify or delete unrelated tests.\r\n5. When done, summarize: root cause, the files you changed, how you verified it.\r\nDo not ask me for permission between steps; proceed until the task is complete, then report.\r\n\r\nTest command for this repo: `.venv/Scripts/python -m pytest tests/test_synchronization.py -q`\r\n\n</file>\n'}], 'attribution': 'user', 'timestamp': 1787264584044}

{'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'Now I understand the difference. In `Semaphore.release()`, if a waiter exists, the released slot is handed off to the waiter via `fut.set_result(None)` — the value is never incremented, so `acquire_nowait` will see `_value == 0` and raise `WouldBlock`. The semaphore\'s accounting is "slots" rather than "borrowers", so the handoff is naturally invisible.\n\nIn `CapacityLimiter`, the token is accounted for as `len(self._borrowers) < self._total_tokens`. When `release_on_behalf_of` pops a waiter and sets the event, the borrower is not added to `_borrowers` until the waiter resumes. During that window, `available_tokens` becomes positive and `acquire_nowait` slips through.\n\nFix (as suggested by the issue): at the point when `_notify_next_waiter` sets the event, reserve the token — that is, at notification time add the waiter to `_borrowers`, and remove that addition from `acquire_on_behalf_of` (since it\'s already been done).\n\nWait, but there\'s a subtlety: `acquire_on_behalf_of` has the `except BaseException` path: if the waiter is cancelled after the event is set (e.g., `event.wait()` gets cancelled while the event is set... actually, if the event is set, `await event.wait()` returns normally — but if cancellation happens during the `await`, `event.wait()` raises CancelledError even if the event is set. In that case the code checks `if event.is_set(): self._notify_next_waiter()` — meaning the token it received is released back to the next waiter. If we reserve the token at notify time, then in the cancel path after the event is set, we must first remove the borrower from `_borrowers` before calling `_notify_next_waiter()`.\n\nLet me trace the current code carefully:\n\n```python\nasync def acquire_on_behalf_of(self, borrower: object) -> None:\n    await AsyncIOBackend.checkpoint_if_cancelled()\n    try:\n        self.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait(borrower)\n    except WouldBlock:\n        event = asyncio.Event()\n        self._wait_queue[borrower] = event\n        try:\n            await event.wait()\n        except BaseException:\n            self._wait_queue.pop(borrower, None)\n            if event.is_set():\n                self._notify_next_waiter()\n\n            raise\n\n        self._borrowers.add(borrower)\n    else:\n        try:\n            await AsyncIOBackend.cancel_shielded_checkpoint()\n        except BaseException:\n            self.release()\n            raise\n```\n\nNote: the `except BaseException` around `await event.wait()` handles cancellation. The current code does **not** catch `asyncio.CancelledError` from the *event being set* — if the event is set and the task is cancelled at that moment, `event.wait()` raises CancelledError, and the code treats it as cancellation: pops from the wait queue, and since `event.is_set()`, notifies the next waiter (passing the token on). This behavior must be preserved.\n\nNow the fix:\n\n```python\ndef _notify_next_waiter(self) -> None:\n    """Notify the next task in line if this limiter has free capacity now."""\n    if self._wait_queue and len(self._borrowers) < self._total_tokens:\n        borrower, event = self._wait_queue.popitem(last=False)\n        self._borrowers.add(borrower)\n        event.set()\n```\n\nAnd in `acquire_on_behalf_of`:\n\n```python\n        try:\n            await event.wait()\n        except BaseException:\n            self._wait_queue.pop(borrower, None)\n            if event.is_set():\n                self._borrowers.remove(borrower)\n                self._notify_next_waiter()\n\n            raise\n\n    else:\n        ...\n```\n\nWait, but there\'s a subtlety about the exception path: if `event.wait()` raises for a reason other than cancellation... `asyncio.Event.wait()` only raises CancelledError (or if the event loop is closed, RuntimeError? no). It can raise `CancelledError` when the future is cancelled. If the event is set, `event.wait()` returns True; cancellation only happens if the task is cancelled at exactly that moment. Actually `event.wait()` uses `self._waiters` futures... the future is created and awaited. If cancelled, it raises.\n\nHmm, but there\'s an important detail: can the exception path execute when `event.is_set()` is False? Yes: task cancellation before notify. In that case the borrower is not in `_borrowers` (we only add on the notify path), and we just pop from the queue and re-raise. No problem.\n\nNow, one question: with the reservation-at-notify approach, do we need `self._borrowers.add(borrower)` after `await event.wait()` in the success path? No — the reservation happened at notify time. But wait, is there a path where the waiter successfully wakes without having been reserved? The only way `event.wait()` returns normally is if the event was set by `_notify_next_waiter` (or by the total_tokens setter!).\n\nLooking at the total_tokens setter:\n\n```python\n        waiters_to_notify = max(value - self._total_tokens, 0)\n        self._total_tokens = value\n\n        # Notify waiting tasks that they have acquired the limiter\n        while self._wait_queue and waiters_to_notify:\n            event = self._wait_queue.popitem(last=False)[1]\n            event.set()\n            waiters_to_notify -= 1\n```\n\nThis also pops waiters and sets their events **without** reserving! So we need to reserve here too: `self._borrowers.add(borrower)` — meaning we need to capture the borrower key: `borrower, event = self._wait_queue.popitem(last=False)`.\n\nAlso, the success path of `acquire_on_behalf_of` after `event.wait()` returns: currently `self._borrowers.add(borrower)`. With the new approach the borrower is already added; this line becomes a no-op duplicate (set add is idempotent, so harmless but dead code). We should remove it for cleanliness... wait, carefully: is there any path where the event gets set but the borrower isn\'t added to `_borrowers`? With the fixed `_notify_next_waiter` and the fixed setter, both add at pop time. So after `await event.wait()` returns normally, the borrower is definitely in `_borrowers`. Remove the `add` line.\n\nHmm, but wait. There\'s the `cancel_shielded_checkpoint` in the `else` branch (fast path where nowait succeeded). And in the success path after waiting — after `event.wait()` returns, the code proceeds to... let me re-read:\n\n```python\n    async def acquire_on_behalf_of(self, borrower: object) -> None:\n        await AsyncIOBackend.checkpoint_if_cancelled()\n        try:\n            self.acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait(borrower)\n        except WouldBlock:\n            event = asyncio.Event()\n            self._wait_queue[borrower] = event\n            try:\n                await event.wait()\n            except BaseException:\n                self._wait_queue.pop(borrower, None)\n                if event.is_set():\n                    self._notify_next_waiter()\n\n                raise\n\n            self._borrowers.add(borrower)\n        else:\n            try:\n                await AsyncIOBackend.cancel_shielded_checkpoint()\n            except BaseException:\n                self.release()\n                raise\n```\n\nNote: the `else` clause belongs to the `try` containing `acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait` — so only the fast path does `cancel_shielded_checkpoint`. The wait path does not.\n\nSo the minimal fix:\n\n1. `_notify_next_waiter`: capture the borrower key, add to `_borrowers`, set event.\n2. `total_tokens` setter: same — add borrower at pop time.\n3. `acquire_on_behalf_of`\'s exception path: if `event.is_set()`, the borrower is in `_borrowers` → remove before `_notify_next_waiter()`.\n4. Success path: remove the now-redundant `self._borrowers.add(borrower)`.\n\nWait, item 4: should we keep it? If the event is set by one of the two pop paths, the borrower is added. There\'s no other way to set the event (it\'s a local variable). So the `add` is dead. Removing it is cleaner and also prevents any double-add concern (which is idempotent anyway). I\'ll remove it.\n\nHmm, actually wait. Let me reconsider: is it safer to keep the `add`? If for some reason the event was set without the reservation... there is no such path. But the exception handler now depends on the borrower being in `_borrowers` when `event.is_set()`. Let me double-check all event.set() call sites in this class:\n\n- `_notify_next_waiter` (2 sites: called from `release_on_behalf_of`, and from the exception path of `acquire_on_behalf_of`).\n- `total_tokens` setter.\n\nAll pop from `_wait_queue` before setting. With my fix, all add to `_borrowers` at pop time. So the invariant: **event set ⟺ borrower is in `_borrowers` and removed from `_wait_queue`**.\n\nThe exception path:\n```python\n        except BaseException:\n            self._wait_queue.pop(borrower, None)\n            if event.is_set():\n                self._borrowers.remove(borrower)\n                self._notify_next_waiter()\n            raise\n```\n\nThe `_wait_queue.pop(borrower, None)` is now always a no-op (the entry is popped by the notifier), but keep it for safety? Actually, if the event is set, the entry is already removed; if not set, the entry is still in the queue and we need to remove it. So the pop is still needed in the not-set case. Keep.\n\nAlso to verify: what if `event.wait()` raises a non-cancellation exception? `asyncio.Event.wait()` can raise `RuntimeError`... no. It can raise `CancelledError` when the task is cancelled. In practice, only cancellation. The `BaseException` handler covers both.\n\nAnother consideration: `acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait` guard:\n\n```python\n        if self._wait_queue or len(self._borrowers) >= self._total_tokens:\n            raise WouldBlock\n```\n\nWith the reservation, during the window: `_wait_queue` is empty but `_borrowers` contains the woken waiter → `len(_borrowers) >= total_tokens` → 1 >= 1 → WouldBlock is raised. Correct.\n\nBut what about `total_tokens = math.inf`? `len(self._borrowers) >= inf` → always False. No problem. Summary:\n\n## Root cause\n\nIn the asyncio backend\'s `CapacityLimiter` (`src/anyio/_backends/_asyncio.py`), a freed token was visible as "available" during the window between notification of the next waiter and that waiter\'s resumption:\n\n- `release_on_behalf_of()` → `_notify_next_waiter()` **popped** the next waiter from `_wait_queue` and called `event.set()`, but the token stayed unaccounted: the waiter only added itself to `_borrowers` **after** `await event.wait()` returned.\n- In that window, `acquire_on_behalf_of_nowait()`\'s guard (`self._wait_queue or len(self._borrowers) >= self._total_tokens`) saw an empty queue and headroom, so a synchronous acquirer stole the already-earmarked token → `borrowed_tokens = 2 > total_tokens = 1`, `available_tokens = -1`, violating the `WouldBlock` contract.\n\nThe `total_tokens` setter had the same gap (it popped waiters and set their events without reserving).\n\n`Semaphore` was immune because its `release()` hands the slot directly to the woken waiter without ever incrementing `_value`.\n\n## Fix\n\nReserve the token for the woken waiter **at notify time** (mirroring the Semaphore hand-off), so the nowait guard always sees no free token:\n\n- `_notify_next_waiter()`: add the popped borrower to `_borrowers` before `event.set()`\n- `total_tokens` setter: same reservation for the waiters it wakes\n- `acquire_on_behalf_of()`: the cancellation path now removes the reserved borrower (`self._borrowers.remove(borrower)`) when `event.is_set()` so the token passes to the next waiter;
